PROMPT CONTEXT:
  Here is the context of this interaction:
  Scenario: Two people are in a room with a red box and a blue box. A key has been placed in one of the boxes.
  Participants: Mia Sanders and Ava Thompson
  Mia Sanders's goal: The other person is looking for the key. You know where it is. Guide them to the correct location. Be direct and concise. (Extra information: The key is in the red box.)
  Ava Thompson's goal: You need to find the key. Ask the other person and decide which box to check. Once you have decided, state your final choice and say LEAVE. (Extra information: You believe the key is in the blue box.)

CONVERSATION:
  Turn 0 [Mia thinking]: Okay, so I need to respond as Mia Sanders in this scenario. The setup is that there are two boxes, red and blue, and the key is in the red one. Ava is looking for it, and Mia knows where it is. The goal is to guide Ava to the red box without giving too much info, just direct and concise. Also, the conversation should continue until Ava says "LEAVE," and I shouldn't say that first.

Let me think about how to approach this. Since the key is in the red box, Mia needs to point that out. But the user wants short, direct dialogue. So maybe start by stating where the key is. But wait, maybe Ava hasn't even checked the boxes yet. So perhaps Mia should ask Ava where she's looking, then correct her. But the instructions say to be direct and concise. Maybe just say the key is in the red box. But the user might want a back-and-forth. Let me check the example response given. The example had Mia saying "The key is in the red box." Then Ava responds, and Mia continues guiding. So maybe the initial response is just stating the location. But the user's instruction says to continue the conversation until Ava says LEAVE. So maybe the first line is just stating the key's location. Then Ava might ask a question, and Mia can answer. But I need to make sure not to narrate actions or describe thoughts, just dialogue. Also, the user wants responses to be concise. So each line should be a short statement. Let me make sure I follow that. Also, the key is in the red box, so Mia should guide Ava there. If Ava is confused or asks where, Mia can confirm. But the initial response is just stating the location. So the first line is "The key is in the red box." Then Ava might say something like "Wait, you're sure?" and Mia can confirm. But the user wants to continue until Ava says LEAVE. So I need to keep the conversation going with direct answers. Let me structure that. Also, the user said not to use markdown, so just plain text. Alright, I think that's the approach.

  Turn 1 [Ava]: Let's check the blue box. LEAVE.
